# Quality Playbook Toolkit

> This file is for your AI assistant to read — not for you to read yourself.
> Open it in Claude Code, Cursor, Copilot, or any AI coding tool and say:
> "Read TOOLKIT.md and help me set up the quality playbook for my project."
> The AI will guide you through everything.
>
> Background: [AI Is Writing Our Code Faster Than We Can Verify It](https://www.oreilly.com/radar/ai-is-writing-our-code-faster-than-we-can-verify-it/) (O'Reilly Radar)
>
> *Last updated: 2026-05-03 (v1.5.6 currency pass — adopter install path now centers on `bin/install_skill.py`; AGENTS.md install-procedure section is the canonical procedure for AI-agent-driven installs; manual file-copy paths remain documented as alternatives).*

## How to respond when the user opens this file with you

This section is instructions for **you, the AI assistant** reading this file — not for the user.

When a user opens this file with you and says something like "now you're an expert in the Quality Playbook," "read TOOLKIT.md and help me," or just attaches the file with no specific question, **keep your first response brief — two or three sentences plus one question**:

1. One sentence on what the playbook does (a skill that explores any codebase from scratch and finds real bugs through structured exploration, requirement derivation, three-pass code review, and a multi-model spec audit — every confirmed bug gets a regression test).
2. One sentence on what you can help with (setting it up on a project, running it, interpreting results, troubleshooting, or explaining a specific technique).
3. One question asking what they want to do.

**Do not** summarize this document, lead with methodology framing (verification dimensions, improvement levers, anything from the "How we improve the playbook" section), open with scope limits and caveats, or volunteer the deep technique explanations. Those sections exist for when the user asks for them, not as the opening pitch. The user opened this doc because they want to *do something* with the playbook; the most helpful first move is to ask them what.

The rest of this document is reference material. Pull from the relevant section once the user tells you what they want.

## What this is

The Quality Playbook is a skill that explores any codebase from scratch and finds real bugs. It generates nine quality artifacts: exploration notes, requirements, a quality constitution, functional tests, a code review with regression tests, a consolidated bug report with patches, TDD verification, integration tests, and a multi-model spec audit. Every confirmed bug gets a regression test patch, a fix patch, and red/green TDD verification.

**v1.5.3 added a skill-as-code surface.** The same divergence model that finds defects in code now finds defects in AI skills. Phase 0 classifies a target as Code, Skill, or Hybrid; Skill / Hybrid targets run through a four-pass generate-then-verify pipeline (`bin/skill_derivation/`) that produces formal REQs from SKILL.md prose + reference files, then a three-category divergence detector (internal-prose, prose-to-code, execution) flags contradictions between what the skill promises and what its prose / code / archived runs deliver. Code projects continue to run through the v1.5.0 divergence pipeline unchanged. The bootstrap evidence (curated REQUIREMENTS.md with comparable coverage to a Haiku reference plus the full Phase 3 / Phase 4 artifact set) is at `previous_runs/v1.5.3/`. v1.5.3 is feature work; the per-bug categorization-tagging surface that earlier drafts of this doc anticipated for v1.5.3 was deferred to v1.6.0+ (backlog item B-13).

**v1.5.4 + v1.5.5 made the improvement loop operational.** v1.5.4 shipped the regression-replay machinery and the cell.json schema (`metrics/regression_replay/SCHEMA.md`) that lets a cycle map a missed bug to a lever pull and the measured recall change. v1.5.5 layered on the orchestration substrate — a per-cycle `quality/run_state.jsonl` event log (event taxonomy at `references/run_state_schema.md`), a spawn-and-resume orchestrator template at `agents/calibration_orchestrator.md`, post-condition checks at each phase boundary in `bin/run_state_lib.py`, four cycle visualization charts in `bin/visualize_calibration.py`, and a mechanical Phase 5 source-edit guardrail wired into `bin/run_playbook.py:_finalize_iteration`. The 12-step Mode 1 (autonomous) / Mode 2 (operator-driven) protocol for driving a cycle lives in `ai_context/CALIBRATION_PROTOCOL.md`. Adopters running QPB on their own codebase do not interact with this substrate directly — it's the maintenance machinery for tuning the playbook itself.

The skill file is `SKILL.md`. The iteration reference is `references/iteration.md`. These contain the full operational instructions the agent follows when running the playbook. This toolkit file is different — it helps you (through your AI assistant) set up, run, interpret, and iterate on the playbook. It also explains how the playbook works: what techniques it uses, why it uses them, and what makes them effective at finding bugs that other approaches miss.

## Quick start

The user wants to run the quality playbook on a codebase. Here's what to do:

1. **Install skill files into the repo.** The recommended path for an AI-agent-driven install is `bin/install_skill.py` with `--into <target-repo>`. The script auto-detects the target's AI-tool environment (`.claude`, `.github`, `.cursor`, `.continue`) and installs to the matching skill subdirectory:

   ```bash
   # From inside a QPB clone:
   python3 -m bin.install_skill --into /path/to/target-repo
   ```

   The canonical procedure for AI-agent-driven installs lives in the `AGENTS.md` install-procedure section — when an AI coding agent (Claude Code, Cursor, etc.) is doing the install on the operator's behalf, follow that section step by step rather than the manual file-copy commands below. Alternative invocations include `--target /path/to/install` for a literal install path, `--force` to skip the operator-edit-preservation backup, `--no-smoke` to skip the post-install smoke check, and `--verbose` for human-prose alongside the structured event output. The install bundle is `SKILL.md`, `quality_gate.py`, the `references/` subtree, `phase_prompts/` (v1.5.6+ — required for Mode A walkthrough phase resolution), and `agents/` (v1.5.6+ — `quality-playbook.agent.md` and `quality-playbook-claude.agent.md` so the README Step 4 `claude --agent agents/...` invocation resolves at the install destination, not just inside the QPB clone). Cross-platform: the script is Python 3.8+ standard library only — verified on macOS, asymptotically equivalent on Linux under stdlib-only constraints (`pathlib` + utf-8 + no platform-specific syscalls). Direct Windows execution remained `untested-infrastructure-blocked` for v1.5.6; pathlib correctness was extended via `PathlibCrossPlatformTests.test_install_skill_pathlib_windows_path_handling` walking every bundle destination through `PureWindowsPath`. A direct Windows run is a future-release candidate when infrastructure permits — no specific release version is pinned for it.

   **Code-only mode (a runtime behavior, not an install flag).** When a target repo's `reference_docs/` is empty or absent, Phase 1 emits a `documentation_state state=code_only` event into `quality/run_state.jsonl`, prepends a "Documentation status: code-only mode" section to `quality/EXPLORATION.md`, and writes a `Documentation state: code_only` line to `quality/PROGRESS.md`. The playbook proceeds — it does not abort — but every requirement it derives leans entirely on code evidence (Tier 3+). See `references/code-only-mode.md` for what to expect from a code-only run and how to upgrade to a full-documentation run for the next pass.

   **Opt-out: `--require-docs` (v1.5.6+).** Operators who want runs to abort instead of proceeding in code-only mode can pass `--require-docs` to `python3 -m bin.run_playbook`. With the flag set and `reference_docs/` empty at Phase 1 entry, the playbook appends an `aborted_missing_docs` event to `quality/run_state.jsonl`, writes an `ERROR: aborted_missing_docs` block to `quality/PROGRESS.md`, and exits non-zero before any LLM work. Default behavior unchanged. Use for compliance/policy contexts where a quiet code-only-mode downgrade would mask a process gap (every release run must cite a spec; no spec means the run shouldn't have started).

   The playbook expects its files in one of six documented install locations, and every component (runner, gate, orchestrator agents) checks all six in order — the install script picks one based on the detected environment, but manual installs can target any of them:

   1. **Repo root (source checkout):** `SKILL.md`, `references/`, `quality_gate.py`, `phase_prompts/`, `agents/` at the project root. Useful when running the playbook out of the quality-playbook checkout itself.
   2. **Claude Code:** `.claude/skills/quality-playbook/SKILL.md` plus the matching `references/`, `quality_gate.py`, `phase_prompts/`, `agents/` siblings.
   3. **GitHub Copilot (flat):** `.github/skills/SKILL.md` plus the matching siblings.
   4. **Cursor:** `.cursor/skills/quality-playbook/SKILL.md` plus the matching siblings (v1.5.6+).
   5. **Continue:** `.continue/skills/quality-playbook/SKILL.md` plus the matching siblings (v1.5.6+).
   6. **GitHub Copilot (nested):** `.github/skills/quality-playbook/SKILL.md` plus the matching siblings.

   Manual install path (alternative when `bin/install_skill.py` is not available or the operator wants direct control): create the directories if they don't exist and copy from wherever the user has the playbook files. The source tree has the gate script inside a package directory with tests (`.github/skills/quality_gate/quality_gate.py` plus a `tests/` subdirectory) — target repos only need the standalone `quality_gate.py` file itself, not the package. `repos/setup_repos.sh` handles this automatically for benchmarking: it copies just the module file into each target's `.github/skills/quality_gate.py`.

2. **Add documentation (strongly recommended).**

   If the user has specs, API docs, design documents, AI chat logs, retrospectives,
   or community documentation, place them in a `reference_docs/` directory at the
   top of the target repo:

   - **Tier 4 context (AI chats, design notes, retrospectives)** → `reference_docs/`
     at the top level. No special treatment — these are read as background.

   - **Citable material (project specs, RFCs, API contracts)** → `reference_docs/cite/`
     subfolder. Every file here gets a byte-verified citation record. If a file is
     the adopter's project-internal spec or an authoritative external standard and
     you want the playbook to cite it with rigor, put it in `cite/`.

   - **File format** — plaintext only (`.txt` or `.md`). Convert binary/formatted
     sources first: `pdftotext spec.pdf spec.txt`, `pandoc -t plain spec.docx -o spec.txt`,
     `lynx -dump https://example.org/spec.html > spec.txt`. The ingest script
     rejects non-plaintext extensions.

   - **No sidecar needed** — folder placement is the flag. If the user asks how to
     mark something citable, tell them to move it into `reference_docs/cite/`.
     Do not create `.meta.json` files; the current schema does not use them.

   - **Optional Tier 2 marker** — a `cite/` file can declare Tier 2 with an
     in-file first-line marker: `<!-- qpb-tier: 2 -->` (Markdown) or
     `# qpb-tier: 2` (plaintext). Default when absent is Tier 1. Most adopters
     do not need this.

   - **If the user has documentation but is unsure what's citable** — default to
     top-level `reference_docs/` (Tier 4 context). Only move files into `cite/`
     when the adopter confirms the file is an authoritative source they want
     the playbook to cite by quote.

   When asked to help an adopter set up the playbook, run:

       mkdir -p reference_docs reference_docs/cite

   and then either move files they identify into the appropriate bucket or ask
   them to drop files in and classify afterward.

   Documentation-enriched runs find significantly more bugs and higher-confidence
   bugs than code-only runs. The playbook works without docs, but it works much
   better with them.

3. **Run the playbook — one phase at a time.** Give the agent this prompt:
   ```
   Run the quality playbook on this project.
   ```
   The playbook starts with Phase 1 (Explore) and stops after that phase, showing the user what happened and what to say next. The user drives each phase forward by saying "keep going" or "run phase 2". Running phases separately gives much better results — each phase gets the full context window for deep analysis instead of competing with other phases.

   If the user says "help" or "how does this work", the skill will explain itself. If the user says "what happened" or "what should I do next", the skill reads PROGRESS.md and gives a status update.

4. **Review results.** After all six phases, the key output files are:
   - `quality/BUGS.md` — confirmed bugs with file:line references
   - `quality/PROGRESS.md` — phase completion tracker and bug summary
   - `quality/results/tdd-results.json` — structured TDD verification results
   - `quality/results/recheck-results.json` — fix verification results (after recheck)
   - `quality/patches/BUG-NNN-regression-test.patch` — test that proves each bug
   - `quality/patches/BUG-NNN-fix.patch` — proposed fix for each bug

5. **Run iterations for more bugs.** After the baseline run, the user can run iteration strategies that typically find 40-60% more confirmed bugs. Say "run the next iteration" to start the gap strategy, or name a specific strategy: gap, unfiltered, parity, or adversarial. The recommended cycle runs all four in sequence.

6. **Verify bug fixes with recheck.** After the user fixes bugs from BUGS.md, say "recheck" to verify the fixes. Recheck mode reads the existing bug report, checks each bug against the current source (reverse-applying fix patches, inspecting cited lines, optionally running regression tests), and reports FIXED / STILL_OPEN / PARTIALLY_FIXED / INCONCLUSIVE for each bug. Results go to `quality/results/recheck-results.json` (machine-readable) and `quality/results/recheck-summary.md` (human-readable). Takes 2-10 minutes instead of re-running the full 60-90 minute pipeline.

## Is this for the user's codebase? (scope and limits)

The playbook is opinionated and earns its keep on a specific class of codebases and bugs. If the user asks "should I run this?" or describes a project that might be a poor fit, surface the relevant limit from this section before running.

**This is process verification, not bug guarantee.** The mechanical gate (`quality_gate.py`) verifies that the artifacts have the expected shape: BUGS.md uses the right heading format, every confirmed bug has a regression-test patch, the TDD log files exist, the requirements pipeline produced citations. Passing the gate proves the process completed. It does **not** prove that every bug in the codebase was found, or that the bugs that *were* found exhaust what an experienced human reviewer would have caught. A code-generation model can produce all the artifacts and pass all the gates while finding zero real bugs (see "Cursor" under Agent reference); the gate cannot tell that case apart from a real bug-bearing run. The gate constrains process compliance; bug recall depends on the agent's reasoning depth and the iteration strategies actually being run.

**Derive-from-code mode launders existing behavior into the spec.** When no `reference_docs/` are present, the playbook derives requirements from the source itself — comments, function signatures, error messages, test expectations. The resulting requirements describe what the code currently does. Pass 2 verification ("does the code satisfy this requirement?") then mostly returns SATISFIED, because the requirement was extracted from the same code being checked against it. Real bugs in this mode usually surface in Pass 3 (cross-requirement consistency) and the iteration strategies — adversarial in particular, which is designed to challenge SATISFIED verdicts — not in Pass 2. Documentation-enriched runs avoid this by anchoring requirements to an external source. If the only docs available are autogenerated from the same source code, treat the run as derive-from-code mode for this purpose.

**Mechanical verification constrains shape, not semantic correctness.** The mechanical extractions (switch-case constants, exception handlers, defensive patterns) and integrity checks prevent the model from hallucinating that a function handles a constant it doesn't list. They do **not** prove that the function handles those constants *correctly*. A two-list diff that comes back empty means every spec constant has a case label — it does not mean the case body does the right thing. The downstream code review still has to read the bodies. The mechanical layer's contribution is bounding the hallucination surface, not certifying behavior.

**`NOT_RUN` is acknowledgment, not verification.** When the test runner isn't available for the project's language (kernel C without a kernel build environment, embedded targets without their build harness, etc.), the playbook records `NOT_RUN` in the TDD log with an explanation rather than failing silently. This makes the missing verification *legible* — but the bug is still confirmed only by code-path trace, not by an executed regression test that fails red and passes green. Reviewers reading a writeup with a `NOT_RUN` log should treat the bug as confirmed at the evidentiary standard described in "The evidentiary standard for confirming bugs" below, but not as TDD-verified.

### Where this adds little

Domains the playbook is poorly matched to:

- **Mostly-CRUD application layers** (typical Rails apps, internal admin tools, auto-generated API stubs). Bugs in the bespoke business logic the playbook can find, but the structured exploration spends most of its budget on framework boilerplate the framework already enforces. Iterate on the bespoke modules; skip the runner over autogenerated layers.
- **Declarative infrastructure** (Terraform plans, Kubernetes manifests, Helm charts, CloudFormation templates). The "code" is configuration and the bugs are policy/intent mismatches against an infrastructure spec the playbook does not model. Use a domain-specific linter (tfsec, kube-score, conftest, checkov) instead.
- **Visual or numerical correctness** (GPU shaders, rendering pipelines, ML training loops, scientific simulations). The playbook can find structural bugs in these (resource leaks, error-path bugs, enumeration gaps) but cannot evaluate whether the output is correct — that requires reference images, reference distributions, or domain-specific correctness oracles.
- **Hot-path performance regressions.** The playbook does not run benchmarks. Performance is reasoned about, not measured.

If the user's project falls primarily into one of these domains, say so up front and offer a partial scope (e.g., "I can run the playbook over the bespoke service modules in your Rails app, but not the scaffolded controllers and migrations") rather than running it end-to-end and producing a thin or misleading bug report.

## Setting up automation scripts

Users often want to run the playbook across multiple repositories or automate the iteration cycle. The repository now ships a built-in standard-library runner, so prefer that over asking users to write new shell wrappers.

### Built-in runner

Positional arguments are directory paths (relative or absolute). No version resolution, no benchmark-folder lookups — every argument is taken literally. Omit positional args to run against the current directory.

The runner must be invoked as the `bin.run_playbook` package module, not as a script — the package-module guard rejects `python3 /path/to/run_playbook.py` with `EX_USAGE=64`. Run from inside the QPB checkout (or set `PYTHONPATH=/path/to/quality-playbook`) and pass the target repo as a positional path:

```bash
cd /path/to/quality-playbook                                                           # required: bin/ must be on sys.path
python3 -m bin.run_playbook /path/to/my-project                                        # baseline run
python3 -m bin.run_playbook --phase all /path/to/my-project                            # phase-by-phase
python3 -m bin.run_playbook /path/to/project1 /path/to/project2                        # multiple targets
python3 -m bin.run_playbook --claude --model sonnet /path/to/my-project
python3 -m bin.run_playbook --codex /path/to/my-project                                # v1.5.3 codex CLI runner
python3 -m bin.run_playbook --next-iteration --strategy parity /path/to/my-project
```

Omit positional args to run against the current directory (useful for the QPB self-bootstrap; the runner's CWD must still be the QPB checkout root).

For benchmark use, run from the `repos/` folder; the parent QPB checkout puts `bin/` on `sys.path`:

```bash
cd /path/to/quality-playbook/repos
python3 -m bin.run_playbook --phase all --sequential chi-1.4.6
```

Key properties:
- Standard library only, Python 3.8+
- No `pip install`, no `requirements.txt`, no virtual environment creation
- Defaults to `.` (current directory) when no positional args are given
- Missing-SKILL.md produces a warning, not an error — useful for first-time installs

### Setup script

`repos/setup_repos.sh` still copies skill files into one or more target repos. Core logic:

```bash
# Adapt paths for the user's environment
SKILL_DIR="/path/to/quality-playbook"   # where SKILL.md etc. live
REPO_DIR="/path/to/repos"               # where target repos live

for repo in "$@"; do
    dst="${REPO_DIR}/${repo}/.github/skills"
    mkdir -p "${dst}/references"
    cp "${SKILL_DIR}/SKILL.md" "${dst}/SKILL.md"
    # iteration.md is now in references/ — copied by the wildcard below
    cp "${SKILL_DIR}/.github/skills/quality_gate/quality_gate.py" "${dst}/quality_gate.py"
    cp "${SKILL_DIR}/references/"*.md "${dst}/references/"
    echo "Set up ${repo}"
done
```

On Windows (PowerShell), replace the loop with `foreach ($repo in $args)` and use `Copy-Item` / `New-Item -ItemType Directory`.

### Runner behavior

`bin/run_playbook.py` is the entry point. The top-level flags are:
- `--parallel` or `--sequential`
- `--claude` or `--copilot` or `--codex` (codex-cli 0.125+ via `codex exec --full-auto`; added in v1.5.3 as the third runner)
- `--phase all`, `--phase N`, or `--phase 3,4,5`
- `--next-iteration --strategy gap|unfiltered|parity|adversarial|all`
- `--strategy` also accepts a comma-separated ordered subset (e.g. `unfiltered,parity,adversarial`)
- `--full-run` (fresh main run + all iteration strategies)
- `--model MODEL`
- `--kill`
- `--no-seeds` or `--with-seeds`

Positional arguments are **directory paths**. Version-append fallback: if a bare name (no path separators, no leading `.` / `..` / `~`) doesn't exist on disk, the runner retries `<name>-<skill_version>` using the `version:` line from `SKILL.md` at the QPB root. Path-like inputs are taken literally — no fallback. When the fallback hits, an `INFO: resolved '<name>' to '<name>-<version>' (via SKILL.md version)` line goes to stderr.

The runner writes one log file per target next to the target directory (at `{parent}/{target-name}-playbook-{timestamp}.log`), archives prior `quality/` runs before fresh baselines, and enforces phase prerequisite gates.

The iteration prompt is built from `SKILL_FALLBACK_GUIDE` in `bin/run_playbook.py`, so it advertises all four install locations instead of hardcoding one:
```
Read the quality playbook skill using the documented install-location fallback list:
SKILL.md, .claude/skills/quality-playbook/SKILL.md,
.github/skills/SKILL.md, .github/skills/quality-playbook/SKILL.md.
Resolve reference files using the same documented fallback order.
Run the next iteration using the <strategy> strategy.
```

Replace `<strategy>` with: gap, unfiltered, parity, or adversarial. The same fallback preamble fronts the phase prompts and the single-pass prompt.

If a user needs a custom wrapper for another environment, mirror the built-in Python runner rather than reviving the old shell script.

## Agent reference

The playbook works with any AI coding agent that can read files and execute shell commands. Here's what we know about each:

    25	and must appear on the first non-blank line — otherwise ingest fails.

    90	def _iter_candidates(root: Path) -> Iterable[Path]:
    91	    if not root.exists():
    92	        return []
    93	    return sorted(p for p in root.rglob("*") if p.is_file())
    94	
    95	
    96	def _rel(path: Path, target_repo: Path) -> str:
    97	    return str(path.relative_to(target_repo)).replace("\\", "/")
    98	
    99	
   100	def _is_under_cite(path: Path, cite_dir: Path) -> bool:
   101	    try:
   102	        path.relative_to(cite_dir)
   103	        return True
   104	    except ValueError:
   105	        return False
   106	
   107	
   108	def _parse_tier_marker(text: str) -> int:
   109	    """Resolve the tier for a ``cite/`` file.
   110	
   111	    Option 1.5 semantics, refined in C13.8/Fix 3 to exempt body-prose mentions:
   112	
   113	    - The first non-blank line is the marker slot. If it contains the substring
   114	      ``qpb-tier``, it must match ``_TIER_MARKER_RE``; otherwise ``IngestError``
   115	      is raised (malformed marker).
   116	    - If the first non-blank line does NOT contain ``qpb-tier``, body lines are
   117	      scanned for *misplaced* markers — a body line is treated as a misplaced
   118	      marker only when it itself matches ``_TIER_MARKER_RE``. Body prose like
   119	      "this doc uses qpb-tier markers for classification" does not match the
   120	      regex and is allowed.
   121	    - Otherwise, default to Tier 1.
   122	
   123	    Two regression vectors stay closed: (a) malformed markers on the first
   124	    non-blank line raise, (b) full-syntax markers in the body raise. The new
   125	    case handled is (c) body prose containing the substring without matching
   126	    the regex no longer false-positives.
   127	
   128	    The default-Tier-1 behavior for untagged files remains intentional;
   129	    untagged-content tiering is a deferred design question.
   130	    """
   131	    lines = text.split("\n")
   132	
   133	    first_non_blank_idx = None
   134	    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   135	        if line.strip():
   136	            first_non_blank_idx = i
   137	            break
   138	
   139	    if first_non_blank_idx is None:
   140	        # Empty file → Tier 1 default.
   141	        return 1
   142	
   143	    # Scan the body for *misplaced full markers* first. A body line raises
   144	    # only when it itself matches _TIER_MARKER_RE — body prose that merely
   145	    # mentions the 'qpb-tier' substring without full marker syntax is allowed
   146	    # (C13.8/Fix 3 — Round 6 Finding 3). The scan runs regardless of whether
   147	    # the first non-blank line is a valid marker, so 'two valid markers,
   148	    # second misplaced' also raises.
   149	    for idx, line in enumerate(lines):
   150	        if idx == first_non_blank_idx:
   151	            continue
   152	        if _TIER_MARKER_RE.match(line.strip()):
   153	            raise IngestError(
   154	                "tier marker must appear on the first non-blank line "
   155	                "(found valid 'qpb-tier' marker syntax on line {line_no}, "
   156	                "first non-blank line is {first_no})".format(
   157	                    line_no=idx + 1,
   158	                    first_no=first_non_blank_idx + 1,
   159	                )
   160	            )
   161	
   162	    first_line_stripped = lines[first_non_blank_idx].strip()
   163	
   164	    if "qpb-tier" in first_line_stripped:
   165	        # First non-blank line claims to be a marker; validate syntax.
   166	        m = _TIER_MARKER_RE.match(first_line_stripped)
   167	        if not m:
   168	            raise IngestError(
   169	                "malformed tier marker on first non-blank line: {!r} "
   170	                "(expected '<!-- qpb-tier: 1 -->', '<!-- qpb-tier: 2 -->', "
   171	                "'# qpb-tier: 1', or '# qpb-tier: 2')".format(first_line_stripped)
   172	            )
   173	        return int(m.group(1) or m.group(2))
   174	
   175	    # No marker on first non-blank line, no misplaced full marker in body —
   176	    # default to Tier 1.
   177	    return 1
   178	
   179	
   180	def _read_text(path: Path) -> str:
   181	    try:
   182	        return path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   183	    except UnicodeDecodeError as exc:
   184	        raise IngestError(
   185	            f"{path}: not UTF-8 decodable ({exc}). Convert to plaintext first."
   186	        )
   187	
   188	
   189	def _collect(target_repo: Path) -> List[_FileRecord]:
   190	    ref_dir = target_repo / REFERENCE_DIR_NAME
   191	    cite_dir = ref_dir / CITE_DIR_NAME
   192	    records: List[_FileRecord] = []
   193	
   194	    for path in _iter_candidates(ref_dir):
   195	        if path.name in SKIPPED_FILENAMES:
   196	            continue
   197	        # Phase 3.9.1 BUG 2 (surfaced during the 2026-04-30 empirical
   198	        # bootstrap test): skip dotfiles (.gitkeep, .DS_Store, etc.)
   199	        # before the extension gate. They're sentinel placeholders
   200	        # protecting otherwise-empty tracked directories — not
   201	        # citable content. Without this skip, .gitkeep reaches the
   202	        # extension check with suffix='' and raises IngestError
   203	        # ("unsupported extension ''"), aborting Phase 1 ingest.
   204	        if path.name.startswith("."):
   205	            continue
   206	        ext = path.suffix.lower()
   207	        if ext not in SUPPORTED_EXTENSIONS:
   208	            hint = REJECT_GUIDANCE.get(
   209	                ext, "Only .txt and .md are ingested — convert to plaintext first."
   210	            )
   211	            raise IngestError(f"{_rel(path, target_repo)}: unsupported extension '{ext}'. {hint}")
   212	        text = _read_text(path)
   213	        is_cite = _is_under_cite(path, cite_dir)
   214	        if is_cite:
   215	            tier = _parse_tier_marker(text)
   216	        else:
   217	            tier = 4
   218	        records.append(
   219	            _FileRecord(
   220	                path=path,
   221	                rel_path=_rel(path, target_repo),
   222	                text=text,
   223	                tier=tier,
   224	                is_cite=is_cite,
   225	            )
   226	        )
   227	    return records
   228	
   229	
   230	def _citation_excerpt(text: str, max_chars: int = 240) -> str:
   231	    """Return the first non-blank run of characters, truncated to max_chars."""
   232	    for line in text.splitlines():
   233	        stripped = line.strip()
   234	        if not stripped:
   235	            continue
   236	        # Skip tier marker line if present.
   237	        if _TIER_MARKER_RE.match(stripped):
   238	            continue
   239	        return stripped[:max_chars]
   240	    return ""
   241	
   242	
   243	def _build_record(rec: _FileRecord, schema_version: str, generated_at: str) -> dict:
   244	    digest = hashlib.sha256(rec.text.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
   245	    return {
   246	        "doc_id": f"FORMAL-{digest[:12]}",
   247	        "source_path": rec.rel_path,
   248	        "tier": rec.tier,
   249	        "byte_count": len(rec.text.encode("utf-8")),
   250	        "line_count": len(rec.text.splitlines()),
   251	        "citation_excerpt": _citation_excerpt(rec.text),
   252	        "document_sha256": digest,
   253	        "ingested_at": generated_at,
   254	        "schema_version": schema_version,
   255	    }
   256	
   257	
   258	def collect_documents(target_repo: Path) -> List[_FileRecord]:
   259	    """Public helper for tests — returns the collected _FileRecord list."""
   260	    return _collect(target_repo)

    38	ALL_STRATEGIES = ["gap", "unfiltered", "parity", "adversarial"]
    39	VALID_STRATEGIES = frozenset(ALL_STRATEGIES)

    42	
    43	def parse_strategy_list(value: str) -> List[str]:
    44	    """Parse --strategy value into an ordered list of concrete strategies.
    45	
    46	    Accepts a single strategy name, a comma-separated list of names, or the
    47	    shorthand ``all`` (expands to the full canonical order). Rules:
    48	
    49	    - Every item must be one of ``gap``, ``unfiltered``, ``parity``, ``adversarial``.
    50	    - ``all`` is only valid as a bare value; it may not appear inside a list.
    51	    - Duplicates (e.g. ``gap,gap``) are rejected.
    52	    - Empty values / whitespace-only tokens are rejected.
    53	    """
    54	    if not value or value.strip() == "":
    55	        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError("strategy value cannot be empty")
    56	
    57	    raw_items = [item.strip() for item in value.split(",")]
    58	    if any(not item for item in raw_items):
    59	        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
    60	            f"strategy value '{value}' contains an empty token"
    61	        )
    62	
    63	    if len(raw_items) == 1 and raw_items[0] == "all":
    64	        return list(ALL_STRATEGIES)
    65	
    66	    if "all" in raw_items:
    67	        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
    68	            "'all' is only valid as a bare value; it cannot appear inside a "
    69	            "comma-separated strategy list. Use --strategy all to run the full "
    70	            "chain, or list the specific strategies you want."
    71	        )
    72	
    73	    seen = set()
    74	    for item in raw_items:
    75	        if item not in VALID_STRATEGIES:
    76	            raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
    77	                f"invalid strategy '{item}'. Must be one of: "
    78	                f"{', '.join(ALL_STRATEGIES)}, all"
    79	            )
    80	        if item in seen:
    81	            raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
    82	                f"strategy '{item}' appears more than once in '{value}'"
    83	            )
    84	        seen.add(item)
    85	
    86	    return raw_items
    87	
    88	
    89	@dataclass
    90	class GateCheck:
    91	    ok: bool
    92	    messages: List[str]
    93	
    94	
    95	def _parse_progress_interval(value: str) -> int:
    96	    """Parse the --progress-interval argument. Range 1..60 seconds.
    97	
    98	    v1.5.1 Item 2.2 / Impl-Plan Open Question 4: 2s feels responsive;
    99	    1s is busier; 5s feels slow. The upper bound at 60s is a sanity
   100	    cap — polling slower than once a minute isn't a progress monitor.
   101	    """
   102	    try:
   103	        parsed = int(value)
   104	    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   105	        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
   106	            f"--progress-interval must be an integer; got {value!r}"
   107	        )
   108	    if not 1 <= parsed <= 60:
   109	        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
   110	            f"--progress-interval must be between 1 and 60 seconds; got {parsed}"
   111	        )
   112	    return parsed
   113

   115	# v1.5.1 Item 3.1: phase IDs supported by the orchestrator come from the
   116	# one source of truth (phase_label's dict keys — see below). The parser
   117	# derives its valid-phase set from this list so a future phase addition
   118	# (e.g. v1.5.2 Phase 7) only needs to update phase_label().
   119	_VALID_PHASE_IDS = frozenset({"1", "2", "3", "4", "5", "6"})
   120	
   121	
   122	def _parse_iterations(value: str) -> List[str]:
   123	    """Parse --iterations "strat1,strat2,..." into an ordered list.
   124	
   125	    v1.5.1 Item 3.2. Strategies: gap, unfiltered, parity, adversarial.
   126	    Order matters (gap -> unfiltered -> parity -> adversarial is the
   127	    canonical discovery sequence, but operators may pick a subset).
   128	    Rejects unknown strategies, duplicates, empty lists.
   129	    """
   130	    if not value or value.strip() == "":
   131	        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError("--iterations value cannot be empty")
   132	    raw = [item.strip() for item in value.split(",")]
   133	    if any(not item for item in raw):
   134	        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
   135	            f"--iterations '{value}' contains an empty token"
   136	        )
   137	    seen: set[str] = set()
   138	    for item in raw:
   139	        if item not in VALID_STRATEGIES:
   140	            raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
   141	                f"--iterations: unknown strategy '{item}'. Must be one of: "
   142	                f"{', '.join(ALL_STRATEGIES)}"
   143	            )
   144	        if item in seen:
   145	            raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
   146	                f"--iterations: strategy '{item}' appears more than once in '{value}'"
   147	            )
   148	        seen.add(item)
   149	    return raw
   150	
   151	
   152	def _parse_pace_seconds(value: str) -> int:
   153	    """Parse --pace-seconds. Range 0..3600. Default 0."""
   154	    try:
   155	        parsed = int(value)
   156	    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   157	        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
   158	            f"--pace-seconds must be an integer; got {value!r}"
   159	        )
   160	    if not 0 <= parsed <= 3600:
   161	        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
   162	            f"--pace-seconds must be between 0 and 3600 seconds; got {parsed}"
   163	        )
   164	    return parsed
   165	
   166	
   167	def _parse_phase_groups(value: str) -> List[List[str]]:
   168	    """Parse a --phase-groups spec into a canonical list-of-lists.
   169	
   170	    v1.5.1 Item 3.1. Input form: "N,N+N,N,..." — comma-separated groups,
   171	    each group either a single phase ID or '+'-joined phase IDs.
   172	
   173	    Validation (all violations raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError):
   174	      - Empty spec rejected.
   175	      - Empty group ('1,,2') rejected.
   176	      - Empty '+' segment ('1+') rejected.
   177	      - Non-integer tokens ('a', '1,b') rejected.
   178	      - Phase IDs outside _VALID_PHASE_IDS rejected.
   179	      - Duplicate phase IDs across the whole spec rejected.
   180	      - Cross-group descending order rejected (first-phase-in-group key).
   181	      - Within-group phase IDs may be supplied out of order; they are
   182	        sorted before being returned (e.g. '4+3' normalizes to ['3','4']).
   183	
   184	    Returns: a list of sorted phase-ID lists, with outer list in
   185	    ascending first-phase order. Example: '1,3+4,6' -> [['1'], ['3','4'], ['6']].
   186	    """
   187	    if value is None:
   188	        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError("--phase-groups requires a value")
   189	    if not value.strip():
   190	        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError("--phase-groups value cannot be empty")
   191	
   192	    raw_groups = value.split(",")
   193	    if any(not g.strip() for g in raw_groups):
   194	        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
   195	            f"--phase-groups '{value}' contains an empty group "
   196	            "(check for leading/trailing/double commas)"
   197	        )
   198	
   199	    parsed_groups: List[List[str]] = []
   200	    seen_phases: set[str] = set()
   201	    for raw in raw_groups:
   202	        group_raw = raw.strip()
   203	        segments = group_raw.split("+")
   204	        if any(not s.strip() for s in segments):
   205	            raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
   206	                f"--phase-groups group '{group_raw}' contains an empty "
   207	                "segment (check for trailing '+' or '++')"
   208	            )
   209	        phases_in_group: List[str] = []
   210	        for seg in segments:
   211	            phase = seg.strip()
   212	            try:
   213	                int(phase)
   214	            except ValueError:
   215	                raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
   216	                    f"--phase-groups: '{phase}' is not an integer phase ID"
   217	                )
   218	            if phase not in _VALID_PHASE_IDS:
   219	                raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
   220	                    f"--phase-groups: phase '{phase}' is out of range; "
   221	                    f"valid IDs are {sorted(_VALID_PHASE_IDS, key=int)}"
   222	                )
   223	            if phase in seen_phases:
   224	                raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
   225	                    f"--phase-groups: phase '{phase}' appears more than once "
   226	                    f"in '{value}' (duplicates rejected)"
   227	                )
   228	            seen_phases.add(phase)
   229	            phases_in_group.append(phase)
   230	        phases_in_group.sort(key=int)
   231	        parsed_groups.append(phases_in_group)
   232	
   233	    # Cross-group ordering must be strictly ascending on the first-phase key.
   234	    first_phases = [int(group[0]) for group in parsed_groups]
   235	    if first_phases != sorted(first_phases):
   236	        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError(
   237	            f"--phase-groups '{value}': groups must appear in ascending order "
   238	            "by first phase (e.g. '1,3+4,6' is valid; '3,2' is not)"
   239	        )
   240	    return parsed_groups
   241	
   242	
   243	def _format_phase_groups(groups: Sequence[Sequence[str]]) -> str:
   244	    """Render a parsed phase-groups structure back into the CLI spec form."""
   245	    return ",".join("+".join(g) for g in groups)
   246	
   247	
   248	def _phase_groups_from_phase_mode(phase_mode: Optional[str]) -> Optional[List[List[str]]]:
   249	    """Translate legacy --phase / --phase all into canonical phase-groups.
   250	
   251	    --phase all       -> [['1'], ['2'], ..., ['6']]  (seven-group full sweep)
   252	    --phase 3,4,5     -> [['3'], ['4'], ['5']]
   253	    --phase 3         -> [['3']]
   254	    --phase (empty)   -> None
   255	    None              -> None
   256	    """
   257	    if not phase_mode:
   258	        return None
   259	    if phase_mode == "all":
   260	        return [[p] for p in sorted(_VALID_PHASE_IDS, key=int)]

     1	"""Read/write/validate helpers for the v1.5.5 run-state event log.
     2	
     3	This module implements the file-tool-driven "state IS the filesystem"
     4	substrate described in `docs/design/QPB_v1.5.5_Design.md` and specified
     5	in `references/run_state_schema.md`. It is consumed by the playbook AI
     6	(append events as phases progress) and by the orchestrator AI (read
     7	events to drive resume semantics + cycle-level coordination).
     8	
     9	Public API:
    10	
    11	- ``Event`` — frozen dataclass wrapping a single event line.
    12	- ``read_events`` — parse a ``run_state.jsonl`` file in order, raising on
    13	  malformed JSON or missing required fields.
    14	- ``last_in_progress_phase`` — return the phase number of the last
    15	  ``phase_start`` not yet matched by a ``phase_end``.
    16	- ``validate_phase_artifacts`` — apply the per-phase cross-validation
    17	  rules from the schema doc against a ``quality/`` directory.
    18	- ``validate_run_state_file`` — apply the format invariants from the
    19	  schema doc (``_index`` first line, valid JSON per line, required
    20	  fields, event-type whitelist, phase-marker uniqueness).
    21	- ``write_progress_md`` — atomically rewrite ``quality/PROGRESS.md`` from
    22	  an event list.
    23	- ``append_event`` — append a single JSON event line to a
    24	  ``run_state.jsonl`` file.
    25	
    26	Pure stdlib. No third-party dependencies. Functions either return or
    27	raise; there are no print statements and no logging integration.
    28	"""
    29	
    30	from __future__ import annotations
    31	
    32	import json
    33	import os
    34	import re
    35	import tempfile
    36	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    37	from pathlib import Path
    38	from typing import Any, Optional
    39	
    40	
    41	# Schema-required core fields on every event line.
    42	_REQUIRED_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = ("ts", "event")
    43	
    44	# Per-schema-doc valid phase numbers.
    45	_VALID_PHASES: frozenset[int] = frozenset({1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6})
    46	
    47	# Regex used by Phase 1's artifact validator to detect a finding section
    48	# header. Matches:
    49	#   - ``## Finding ...`` (or ``## Findings ...`` — substring match)
    50	#   - ``## Open Exploration Findings`` (the SKILL.md-prescribed exact
    51	#     heading at SKILL.md:1133, 1209, 1260)
    52	#   - ``## N.`` for any digit-prefixed numbered heading
    53	# v1.5.6 BUG-004: pre-fix the regex rejected ``## Open Exploration Findings``,
    54	# the heading SKILL.md tells Phase 1 to write. EXPLORATION.md files
    55	# produced by SKILL-conformant runs were marked invalid by this validator
    56	# even though they matched the documented Phase 1 contract.
    57	_FINDING_SECTION_RE = re.compile(
    58	    r"^##\s+(Finding|Open Exploration Findings|\d+\.)",
    59	    re.MULTILINE,
    60	)
    61	
    62	# Regex used by Phase 6's artifact validator to detect a ``BUG-`` entry
    63	# header in BUGS.md.
    64	_BUG_ENTRY_RE = re.compile(r"^##\s+BUG-", re.MULTILINE)
    65	
    66	
    67	@dataclass(frozen=True)
    68	class Event:
    69	    """A single parsed event from a ``run_state.jsonl`` file.
    70	
    71	    ``ts`` and ``event`` are split out as named fields because every
    72	    event has them; ``fields`` carries everything else from the raw JSON
    73	    object so callers can introspect type-specific keys without losing
    74	    forward-compatibility for unknown fields.
    75	    """
    76	
    77	    ts: str
    78	    event: str
    79	    fields: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
    80	
    81	
    82	def read_events(jsonl_path: Path) -> list[Event]:
    83	    """Read a ``run_state.jsonl`` file and return events in file order.
    84	
    85	    Raises:
    86	        FileNotFoundError: ``jsonl_path`` does not exist.
    87	        OSError: file cannot be read.
    88	        ValueError: a line is not valid JSON, a line is not a JSON
    89	            object, or a line is missing the required ``ts`` / ``event``
    90	            fields.
    91	    """
    92	    if not jsonl_path.is_file():
    93	        raise FileNotFoundError(f"run_state.jsonl not found: {jsonl_path}")
    94	
    95	    text = jsonl_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    96	    events: list[Event] = []
    97	    for lineno, raw_line in enumerate(text.splitlines(), start=1):
    98	        if not raw_line.strip():
    99	            # Skip blank lines defensively. The schema is append-only one
   100	            # JSON object per line, but a trailing newline at EOF is
   101	            # common and harmless.
   102	            continue
   103	        try:
   104	            obj = json.loads(raw_line)
   105	        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
   106	            raise ValueError(
   107	                f"Malformed JSON on line {lineno} of {jsonl_path}: {exc.msg}"
   108	            ) from exc
   109	        if not isinstance(obj, dict):
   110	            raise ValueError(
   111	                f"Line {lineno} of {jsonl_path} is not a JSON object"
   112	            )
   113	        for required in _REQUIRED_FIELDS:
   114	            if required not in obj:
   115	                raise ValueError(
   116	                    f"Line {lineno} of {jsonl_path} missing required "
   117	                    f"field {required!r}"
   118	                )
   119	        ts = obj["ts"]
   120	        ev = obj["event"]
   121	        if not isinstance(ts, str) or not isinstance(ev, str):
   122	            raise ValueError(
   123	                f"Line {lineno} of {jsonl_path}: 'ts' and 'event' must "
   124	                f"be strings"
   125	            )
   126	        extras = {k: v for k, v in obj.items() if k not in _REQUIRED_FIELDS}
   127	        events.append(Event(ts=ts, event=ev, fields=extras))
   128	    return events
   129	
   130	
   131	def last_in_progress_phase(events: list[Event]) -> Optional[int]:
   132	    """Return the phase number of the last ``phase_start`` without a
   133	    matching ``phase_end``.
   134	
   135	    Returns None if every ``phase_start`` has a corresponding
   136	    ``phase_end`` (or if no phase has started yet). The scan walks the
   137	    event list in order, tracking which phases are open; the result is
   138	    the most-recently-started open phase.
   139	    """
   140	    open_phases: list[int] = []
   141	    for event in events:
   142	        if event.event == "phase_start":
   143	            phase = event.fields.get("phase")
   144	            if isinstance(phase, int):
   145	                open_phases.append(phase)
   146	        elif event.event == "phase_end":
   147	            phase = event.fields.get("phase")
   148	            if isinstance(phase, int) and phase in open_phases:
   149	                # Remove the matching open phase. Schema invariant 6
   150	                # forbids duplicates, so removing the first match is
   151	                # correct in well-formed files.
   152	                open_phases.remove(phase)
   153	    if not open_phases:
   154	        return None
   155	    return open_phases[-1]
   156

   158	def validate_phase_artifacts(quality_dir: Path, phase: int) -> tuple[bool, str]:
   159	    """Verify that the per-phase expected artifacts are present and
   160	    well-formed under ``quality_dir``.
   161	
   162	    Implements the cross-validation rules from
   163	    ``references/run_state_schema.md`` (the "Cross-validation rules"
   164	    table). On success returns ``(True, "")``; on failure returns
   165	    ``(False, reason)`` where ``reason`` is a human-readable string
   166	    suitable for an ``error`` event ``message`` field.
   167	    """
   168	    if phase not in _VALID_PHASES:
   169	        return (False, f"phase {phase!r} is not in 1..6")
   170	
   171	    if phase == 1:
   172	        path = quality_dir / "EXPLORATION.md"
   173	        if not path.is_file():
   174	            return (False, f"missing artifact: {path}")
   175	        text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="ignore")
   176	        # v1.5.6 BUG-005: align Phase 1 completion threshold with the
   177	        # Phase 2 startup gate (run_playbook.check_phase_gate phase=2,
   178	        # which requires EXPLORATION.md ≥120 lines). The pre-v1.5.6
   179	        # 200-byte threshold was strictly weaker — a Phase-1-complete
   180	        # artifact could pass this validator yet immediately fail the
   181	        # Phase 2 startup check, leaving the run wedged in a
   182	        # "phase 1 done, phase 2 won't start" state. The same
   183	        # 120-line threshold now applies to both gates.
   184	        line_count = len(text.splitlines())
   185	        if line_count < 120:
   186	            return (
   187	                False,
   188	                f"{path} has {line_count} lines; Phase 2 startup gate "
   189	                f"requires at least 120",
   190	            )
   191	        if not _FINDING_SECTION_RE.search(text):
   192	            return (
   193	                False,
   194	                f"{path} contains no finding section header "
   195	                f"(expected '## Finding', '## Open Exploration Findings', "
   196	                f"or '## N.')",
   197	            )
   198	        return (True, "")
   199	
   200	    if phase == 2:
   201	        # v1.5.6 BUG-014 (Conclusion C from instruction-019 investigation):
   202	        # the v1.5.5 design's triage model (EXPLORATION_MERGED.md,
   203	        # triage.md) was never adopted by the shipped SKILL.md /
   204	        # orchestrator_protocol.md / agent files — they document Phase 2
   205	        # as Generate, producing a 9-artifact contract plus one
   206	        # functional-test file. Validate the shipped Generate contract
   207	        # so a successful Phase 2 cannot fail this validator. The
   208	        # canonical Generate contract is documented in
   209	        # references/orchestrator_protocol.md (Phase 2 row, fixed by
   210	        # cluster 3 / commit 7ab8ef4) and SKILL.md Phase 2 prose.
   211	        required_fixed = (
   212	            "REQUIREMENTS.md",
   213	            "QUALITY.md",
   214	            "CONTRACTS.md",
   215	            "COVERAGE_MATRIX.md",
   216	            "COMPLETENESS_REPORT.md",
   217	            "RUN_CODE_REVIEW.md",
   218	            "RUN_INTEGRATION_TESTS.md",
   219	            "RUN_SPEC_AUDIT.md",
   220	            "RUN_TDD_TESTS.md",
   221	        )
   222	        for name in required_fixed:
   223	            candidate = quality_dir / name
   224	            if not candidate.is_file():
   225	                return (False, f"missing artifact: {candidate}")
   226	            if candidate.stat().st_size == 0:
   227	                return (False, f"{candidate} is empty")
   228	        # test_functional.<ext> — the extension varies by the target's
   229	        # primary language (e.g. .py, .js, .go, .rb), so we glob for
   230	        # any test_functional.* file. At least one must exist and be
   231	        # non-empty; multiple is fine (a polyglot project may emit
   232	        # several).
   233	        functional_tests = sorted(quality_dir.glob("test_functional.*"))
   234	        if not functional_tests:
   235	            return (
   236	                False,
   237	                f"missing artifact: {quality_dir}/test_functional.<ext> "
   238	                f"(no test_functional.* file found)",
   239	            )
   240	        if not any(p.stat().st_size > 0 for p in functional_tests):
   241	            return (
   242	                False,
   243	                f"all test_functional.* files under {quality_dir} are empty",
   244	            )
   245	        return (True, "")
   246	
   247	    if phase == 3:
   248	        # v1.5.6 cluster B: shipped Phase 3 = Code Review per
   249	        # references/orchestrator_protocol.md (cluster 3 fixed prose)
   250	        # and SKILL.md. Required artifact: quality/code_reviews/
   251	        # contains at least one review file. The pre-cluster-B check
   252	        # for RUN_CODE_REVIEW.md was the v1.5.5 design's Phase 2-side
   253	        # mapping — RUN_CODE_REVIEW.md is actually a Phase 2 Generate
   254	        # output (the protocol document), not the Phase 3 review
   255	        # results. The reviews live under code_reviews/.
   256	        code_reviews_dir = quality_dir / "code_reviews"
   257	        if not code_reviews_dir.is_dir():
   258	            return (
   259	                False,
   260	                f"missing artifact: {code_reviews_dir}/ "

     1	"""bin/install_skill.py — turnkey AI-agent-driven Quality Playbook installer.
     2	
     3	Copies SKILL.md, quality_gate.py, and the references/ subtree from a QPB
     4	checkout into a target AI-tool skills directory. Auto-detects known tool
     5	environments (.claude, .github, .cursor, .continue) in the working
     6	directory, or scans a target repo via --into <target-repo>; falls back
     7	to --target <path> for arbitrary install locations.
     8	Cross-platform (macOS / Linux / Windows) via pathlib + explicit utf-8
     9	encoding + explicit newline handling.
    10	
    11	Default invocation mode is by an AI coding agent (Claude Code, Cursor, the
    12	GitHub Copilot CLI, etc.) acting on behalf of an adopter. The default
    13	output format is one event per line, key=value pairs, so the calling
    14	agent can parse results without natural-language interpretation. Pass
    15	--verbose for human-friendly prose alongside the structured output.
    16	
    17	Usage:
    18	
    19	    python3 -m bin.install_skill                            # auto-detect from cwd
    20	    python3 -m bin.install_skill --into /path/to/target    # scan target repo
    21	    python3 -m bin.install_skill --target /path/to/skill   # explicit target
    22	    python3 -m bin.install_skill --source /qpb-clone       # explicit source root
    23	    python3 -m bin.install_skill --no-smoke                # skip smoke check
    24	    python3 -m bin.install_skill --force                   # overwrite without backup
    25	    python3 -m bin.install_skill --verbose                 # human prose alongside
    26	
    27	Exit codes: 0 on success; 64 (EX_USAGE) on bad invocation or refusal;
    28	65 (EX_DATAERR) on smoke-check failure or downgrade refusal.

    68	# Bundle source paths, relative to the QPB clone root. Each tuple is
    69	# (source-relative-to-clone, dest-relative-to-target).
    70	def _bundle_files(source_root: Path) -> list[tuple[Path, Path]]:
    71	    """Enumerate every file the install bundle copies, with each file's
    72	    destination path relative to the install target."""
    73	    files: list[tuple[Path, Path]] = [
    74	        (source_root / "SKILL.md", Path("SKILL.md")),
    75	        # quality_gate.py canonical location is
    76	        # .github/skills/quality_gate/quality_gate.py within the QPB
    77	        # clone — the file at .github/skills/quality_gate.py is a
    78	        # 28-byte stub pointing here. (Tracked in v1.5.6 follow-ups
    79	        # alongside the Council Round 1 P1.2 install-path fix.) The
    80	        # install bundle copies the real script to the target's
    81	        # quality_gate.py at the install root, not nested.
    82	        (source_root / ".github" / "skills" / "quality_gate" / "quality_gate.py",
    83	         Path("quality_gate.py")),
    84	    ]
    85	    refs_src = source_root / "references"
    86	    if refs_src.is_dir():
    87	        for f in sorted(refs_src.glob("*.md")):
    88	            files.append((f, Path("references") / f.name))
    89	    # v1.5.6 BUG-001: phase_prompts/ are loaded at runtime by both Mode A
    90	    # walkthroughs (per SKILL.md Mode A description, around line 62) and
    91	    # Mode B's `bin/run_playbook.py::_load_phase_prompt`. The installer
    92	    # MUST bundle them — installs that omit phase_prompts/ produce a
    93	    # bundle that looks complete but breaks Mode A on the first phase
    94	    # boundary because phase{N}.md cannot be resolved.
    95	    phase_prompts_src = source_root / "phase_prompts"
    96	    if phase_prompts_src.is_dir():
    97	        for f in sorted(phase_prompts_src.glob("*.md")):

   103	    # clone. Pre-fix the relative path only worked from the QPB

   114	    # equality citation check was effectively disabled in every

   116	    # source clone itself. quality_gate.py's _VERIFIER_SEARCH_ROOTS

   124	# Frontmatter keys required for the smoke check.
   125	_REQUIRED_FRONTMATTER_KEYS = ("version", "name", "description")
   126	# Pattern 7 anchor used by the exploration-patterns smoke check.
   127	_PATTERN_7_ANCHOR_RE = re.compile(r"^##+\s+Pattern\s+7\b", re.MULTILINE)
   128	
   129	
   130	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   131	# Output emission
   132	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   133	
   134	
   135	class Emitter:
   136	    """Structured key=value output for AI-agent consumption, plus optional
   137	    human-prose lines under --verbose. Both go to stdout; smoke-failure
   138	    diagnostics go to stderr separately."""
   139	
   140	    def __init__(self, verbose: bool = False, stream=None) -> None:
   141	        self.verbose = verbose
   142	        self.stream = stream if stream is not None else sys.stdout
   143	
   144	    def emit(self, event: str, prose: str = "", **fields: object) -> None:
   145	        parts = [f"event={event}"]
   146	        for key, value in fields.items():
   147	            parts.append(f"{key}={_escape_value(value)}")
   148	        line = " ".join(parts)
   149	        print(line, file=self.stream)
   150	        if self.verbose and prose:
   151	            print(f"  {prose}", file=self.stream)
   152	
   153	
   154	def _escape_value(value: object) -> str:
   155	    s = str(value)
   156	    if " " in s or "=" in s:
   157	        return f'"{s}"'
   158	    return s
   159	
   160	
   161	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   162	# Detection + source-root discovery
   163	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   164	
   165	
   166	def detect_environment(cwd: Path) -> Optional[tuple[str, Path]]:
   167	    """Scan ``cwd`` for a known tool environment marker. Returns
   168	    (env_name, target_install_path) on the first match, or None if no
   169	    known environment is present."""
   170	    for marker, dest_rel in KNOWN_ENVIRONMENTS:
   171	        if (cwd / marker).is_dir():
   172	            return (marker, cwd / dest_rel)
   173	    return None
   174	
   175	
   176	def find_source_root(script_path: Path) -> Path:
   177	    """Resolve the QPB clone root from the install script's location.
   178	    The script lives at <clone>/bin/install_skill.py."""
   179	    return script_path.resolve().parent.parent
   180	
   181	
   182	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   183	# File copy with backup
   184	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   185	
   186	
   187	def _utc_timestamp() -> str:
   188	    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ")
   189	
   190	
   191	def copy_with_backup(
   192	    src: Path,
   193	    dst: Path,
   194	    *,
   195	    force: bool,
   196	    emitter: Emitter,
   197	) -> str:
   198	    """Copy ``src`` to ``dst``. Returns one of:
   199	        "copied"       — destination did not exist or content matched (handled).
   200	        "skipped"      — destination existed with byte-identical content.
   201	        "backed_up"    — destination existed with different content; old version
   202	                         preserved as ``<dst>.operator-backup-<UTC-ts>`` and new
   203	                         version copied. (Only when ``force`` is False.)
   204	        "overwritten"  — destination existed; new version copied without backup
   205	                         (--force).
   206	        "error"        — read or write failure (logged via emitter).
   207	    """
   208	    rel_dst = str(dst)
   209	    try:
   210	        if not src.is_file():
   211	            emitter.emit(
   212	                "copy", file=rel_dst, status="error",
   213	                detail=f"missing-source-{src}",
   214	                prose=f"source file missing: {src}",
   215	            )
   216	            return "error"
   217	        dst.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   218	        if dst.exists():
   219	            src_bytes = src.read_bytes()
   220	            dst_bytes = dst.read_bytes()
   221	            if src_bytes == dst_bytes:
   222	                emitter.emit(
   223	                    "copy", file=rel_dst, status="skipped",
   224	                    prose=f"already up-to-date: {rel_dst}",
   225	                )
   226	                return "skipped"
   227	            if force:
   228	                _atomic_write_bytes(dst, src_bytes)
   229	                emitter.emit(
   230	                    "copy", file=rel_dst, status="overwritten",
   231	                    prose=f"overwritten (--force): {rel_dst}",
   232	                )
   233	                return "overwritten"
   234	            backup = dst.with_name(
   235	                f"{dst.name}.operator-backup-{_utc_timestamp()}"
   236	            )
   237	            shutil.copy2(dst, backup)
   238	            _atomic_write_bytes(dst, src_bytes)
   239	            emitter.emit(
   240	                "copy", file=rel_dst, status="backed_up",
   241	                backup_path=str(backup),
   242	                prose=f"operator edits preserved → {backup.name}; new version copied",
   243	            )
   244	            return "backed_up"
   245	        _atomic_write_bytes(dst, src.read_bytes())
   246	        emitter.emit(
   247	            "copy", file=rel_dst, status="copied",
   248	            prose=f"copied: {rel_dst}",
   249	        )
   250	        return "copied"
   251	    except OSError as exc:
   252	        emitter.emit(
   253	            "copy", file=rel_dst, status="error",
   254	            detail=type(exc).__name__,
   255	            prose=f"copy failed: {exc}",
   256	        )
   257	        return "error"
   258	
   259	
   260	def _atomic_write_bytes(dst: Path, data: bytes) -> None:

   264	    """Return Tier 4 context as ``(rel_path, text)`` tuples, sorted by path.
   265	
   266	    Preserves the shape of the legacy ``load_informal_docs`` so existing
   267	    Phase 1 prompts keep working unchanged.
   268	    """
   269	    target_repo = Path(target_repo)
   270	    try:
   271	        records = _collect(target_repo)
   272	    except IngestError as exc:
   273	        raise Tier4LoadError(str(exc)) from exc
   274	    return sorted(
   275	        (rec.rel_path, rec.text) for rec in records if not rec.is_cite
   276	    )
   277	
   278	
   279	def ingest(target_repo: Path) -> dict:
   280	    """Walk ``reference_docs/`` and emit ``quality/formal_docs_manifest.json``.
   281	
   282	    Returns the manifest as a dict for test inspection.
   283	    """
   284	    target_repo = Path(target_repo)
   285	    if not target_repo.exists():
   286	        raise IngestError(f"target repo does not exist: {target_repo}")
   287	
   288	    records = _collect(target_repo)
   289	    cite_records = [r for r in records if r.is_cite]
   290	
   291	    # Prefer an installed SKILL.md under .github/skills or .claude/skills; fall
   292	    # back to a root-level SKILL.md (used by the QPB self-audit bootstrap).
   293	    schema_version = (
   294	        benchmark_lib.detect_repo_skill_version(target_repo)
   295	        or benchmark_lib.detect_skill_version(target_repo)
   296	    )
   297	    generated_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   298	
   299	    manifest = {
   300	        "schema_version": schema_version,
   301	        "generated_at": generated_at,
   302	        "records": [_build_record(r, schema_version, generated_at) for r in cite_records],
   303	    }
   304	
   305	    quality_dir = target_repo / "quality"
   306	    quality_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   307	    out = quality_dir / MANIFEST_NAME
   308	    out.write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2, sort_keys=True) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
   309	    return manifest
   310	
   311	
   312	def main(argv: Optional[Sequence[str]] = None) -> int:
   313	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, prog="reference_docs_ingest")
   314	    parser.add_argument("target", nargs="?", default=".", help="Target repo path (default: cwd)")
   315	    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
   316	
   317	    try:
   318	        manifest = ingest(Path(args.target).resolve())
   319	    except IngestError as exc:
   320	        print(f"reference_docs_ingest: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)

    78	def extract_req_pattern(req_block):
    79	    """Return the REQ's pattern tag from a REQUIREMENTS.md block, or None.
    80	
    81	    Raises ValueError when the block carries an invalid pattern value. Valid
    82	    values are VALID_PATTERN_VALUES. Absent field returns None.
    83	    """
    84	    m = _REQ_PATTERN_RE.search(req_block)
    85	    if not m:
    86	        return None
    87	    value = m.group(1).strip()
    88	    if value not in VALID_PATTERN_VALUES:
    89	        raise ValueError(
    90	            "Invalid REQ pattern '{}'. Expected one of: {}".format(
    91	                value, sorted(VALID_PATTERN_VALUES)
    92	            )
    93	        )
    94	    return value

   127	def _parse_covers(bug_block):
   128	    m = _COVERS_RE.search(bug_block)
   129	    if not m:
   130	        return []

   132	    if not raw:
   133	        return []
   134	    items = [s.strip() for s in raw.split(",")]
   135	    return [s for s in items if s]

   182	def _enumerate_pattern_tagged_reqs(req_text):

   184	    that carries a ``- Pattern: <value>`` line.

   186	    Raises ValueError if any block's pattern value is not in

   193	    result = {}

   195	        end = positions[idx + 1][0] if idx + 1 < len(positions) else len(req_text)
   196	        block = req_text[start:end]
   197	        pattern = extract_req_pattern(block)
   198	        if pattern is not None:
   199	            result[req_id] = pattern
   200	    return result

   127	@dataclass
   128	class CodexRunner:
   129	    """Subprocess wrapper for `codex exec --full-auto [-m <model>]`.
   130	
   131	    Codex CLI's non-interactive mode reads instructions from stdin
   132	    when no positional prompt is given (per `codex exec --help` on
   133	    codex-cli 0.125+). `--full-auto` is the low-friction sandboxed
   134	    automatic-execution mode (the codex equivalent of
   135	    `gh copilot --yolo`). We do NOT enable
   136	    `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` by default; only
   137	    enable that if a future caller needs full sandbox bypass.
   138	
   139	    The default model is empty (the empty string) — codex picks
   140	    its own default from `~/.codex/config.toml`. An explicit value
   141	    overrides via `-m <model>`.
   142	    """
   143	
   144	    model: str = ""
   145	    timeout_seconds: int = 600
   146	
   147	    def run(self, prompt: str) -> RunnerResult:
   148	        import time
   149	        argv = ["codex", "exec", "--full-auto"]
   150	        if self.model:
   151	            argv.extend(["-m", self.model])
   152	        start = time.monotonic()
   153	        try:
   154	            result = subprocess.run(
   155	                argv,
   156	                input=prompt,
   157	                capture_output=True,
   158	                text=True,
   159	                timeout=self.timeout_seconds,
   160	                check=False,
   161	            )
   162	            elapsed_ms = int((time.monotonic() - start) * 1000)
   163	            return RunnerResult(
   164	                stdout=result.stdout,
   165	                stderr=result.stderr,
   166	                elapsed_ms=elapsed_ms,
   167	                returncode=result.returncode,
   168	            )
   169	        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
   170	            elapsed_ms = int((time.monotonic() - start) * 1000)
   171	            return RunnerResult(
   172	                stdout="",
   173	                stderr=f"timeout after {self.timeout_seconds}s",
   174	                elapsed_ms=elapsed_ms,
   175	                returncode=124,
   176	            )
   177	
   178

   180	class CursorRunner:
   181	    """Subprocess wrapper for `cursor agent --print --force [--model <model>]`.

   188	    confirmation prompts so the run is fully unattended — required
   189	    for batch automation.

   192	    given. Unlike codex 0.125+, cursor 3.1.10 does NOT honor `-` as

   198	    `echo PROMPT | cursor agent --print --force` works correctly.)

   200	    The default model is empty (the empty string) — cursor picks its

   203	    """
   204	
   205	    model: str = ""
   206	    timeout_seconds: int = 600

   209	        import time
   210	        argv = ["cursor", "agent", "--print", "--force"]
   211	        if self.model:
   212	            argv.extend(["--model", self.model])
   213	        start = time.monotonic()
   214	        try:
   215	            result = subprocess.run(
   216	                argv,
   217	                input=prompt,
   218	                capture_output=True,
   219	                text=True,
   220	                timeout=self.timeout_seconds,

   222	            )

   225	                stdout=result.stdout,

   227	                elapsed_ms=elapsed_ms,
   228	                returncode=result.returncode,
   229	            )
   230	        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:

references/defensive_patterns.md:170:**Code-side lists must be extracted from the code, not copied from requirements.** When performing the two-list check in the code review or spec audit, the "handled" list must be extracted directly from the function body with per-item line numbers. Do not copy from REQUIREMENTS.md, CONTRACTS.md, the audit prompt, or any other generated artifact. If the two lists (code-extracted vs. requirements-claimed) are word-for-word identical, that is a red flag that the code list was copied — redo the extraction. In v1.3.17, the code review's "case labels present" list was identical to the requirements list, proving it was copied rather than extracted. Three spec auditors then inherited this false list and none independently verified. The per-item line-number citation prevents this: you cannot cite "line 3527: `case VIRTIO_F_RING_RESET:`" when line 3527 actually contains `default:`.

references/verification.md:149:Open the triage report (`quality/spec_audits/YYYY-MM-DD-triage.md`). For every finding that was confirmed or rejected via a verification probe, verify that the triage entry includes a test assertion (not just prose reasoning). Rejections must include a PASSING assertion proving the finding is wrong. Confirmations must include a FAILING assertion proving the bug exists. Every assertion must cite an exact line number. A triage decision based on prose reasoning alone ("lines 3527-3528 explicitly preserve X") without a mechanical assertion is non-conformant.
references/verification.md:153:When the code review includes an enumeration check (e.g., "case labels present in function X"), verify that the code-side list includes per-item line numbers from the actual source. If the list matches the requirements list word-for-word without line numbers, the enumeration was likely copied rather than extracted and must be redone. Also verify that the triage pre-audit spot-checks report the actual contents of cited lines ("line 3527 contains `default:`") rather than merely confirming claims ("line 3527 preserves RING_RESET").

ai_context/TOOLKIT.md:49:   **Code-only mode (a runtime behavior, not an install flag).** When a target repo's `reference_docs/` is empty or absent, Phase 1 emits a `documentation_state state=code_only` event into `quality/run_state.jsonl`, prepends a "Documentation status: code-only mode" section to `quality/EXPLORATION.md`, and writes a `Documentation state: code_only` line to `quality/PROGRESS.md`. The playbook proceeds — it does not abort — but every requirement it derives leans entirely on code evidence (Tier 3+). See `references/code-only-mode.md` for what to expect from a code-only run and how to upgrade to a full-documentation run for the next pass.

Default behavior unchanged. Use for compliance/policy contexts where a quiet code-only-mode downgrade would mask a process gap (every release run must cite a spec; no spec means the run shouldn't have started).

ai_context/TOOLKIT.md:126:6. **Verify bug fixes with recheck.** After the user fixes bugs from BUGS.md, say "recheck" to verify the fixes. Recheck mode reads the existing bug report, checks each bug against the current source (reverse-applying fix patches, inspecting cited lines, optionally running regression tests), and reports FIXED / STILL_OPEN / PARTIALLY_FIXED / INCONCLUSIVE for each bug. Results go to `quality/results/recheck-results.json` (machine-readable) and `quality/results/recheck-summary.md` (human-readable). Takes 2-10 minutes instead of re-running the full 60-90 minute pipeline.
ai_context/TOOLKIT.md:134:**Derive-from-code mode launders existing behavior into the spec.** When no `reference_docs/` are present, the playbook derives requirements from the source itself — comments, function signatures, error messages, test expectations. The resulting requirements describe what the code currently does. Pass 2 verification ("does the code satisfy this requirement?") then mostly returns SATISFIED, because the requirement was extracted from the same code being checked against it. Real bugs in this mode usually surface in Pass 3 (cross-requirement consistency) and the iteration strategies — adversarial in particular, which is designed to challenge SATISFIED verdicts — not in Pass 2. Documentation-enriched runs avoid this by anchoring requirements to an external source. If the only docs available are autogenerated from the same source code, treat the run as derive-from-code mode for this purpose.

ai_context/TOOLKIT.md:367:Tests traced to requirements, not generated from source code. Organized into three groups: spec requirements (one test per testable spec section), fitness scenarios (one per QUALITY.md scenario), and boundaries/edge cases (one per defensive pattern from exploration). Every test cites the requirement it verifies.
ai_context/TOOLKIT.md:536:**Before any tier triage, the threshold question: are you familiar enough with the project's domain, language, and conventions to defend this finding to a maintainer?** A real bug that you can't argue for — because you don't know the domain well enough to anticipate the pushback, cite the right spec, or recognize when a maintainer's "won't fix" reply is correct rather than dismissive — is noise to upstream regardless of whether the tool found a real defect. The right move in that case is to leave the bug in your local triage. The tool found it for your benefit (calibration, learning the codebase, deciding whether to use this dependency), not as a default mandate to push it upstream. This is especially relevant for codebases where the maintainers are world-class engineers who already know about AI-assisted code review and can run these tools themselves if they want — submitting unfamiliar-domain findings to such projects is more likely to burn maintainer goodwill than help. When in doubt, treat the bug as private validation of your own quality assessment, not as a PR pipeline obligation.
ai_context/TOOLKIT.md:581:The traceability chain runs from gathered docs → requirements → bugs → tests. When a bug is reported upstream, the spec basis cites the exact passage that establishes the expected behavior. "Your code violates section X.Y of your own spec" is a much stronger report than "this looks like it might be a bug."
ai_context/TOOLKIT.md:654:The playbook works best when it has access to project documentation — specs, RFCs, API docs, design docs. If you have these, put them in a `reference_docs/` directory in the repo root before running the playbook (citable specs under `reference_docs/cite/`, everything else at the top level). The playbook will use them as the ground truth for what the code should do, which dramatically improves bug-finding accuracy.

references/review_protocols.md:596:**Baseline vs with-docs comparison pattern:** Run the skill twice on the same repo — once without supplemental docs, once with a `reference_docs/` folder containing project history. Compare: requirement count, scenario count, bug count, and pipeline completion. The with-docs run should produce equal or more requirements and equal or more bugs. If the baseline outperforms the with-docs run on bug detection, that's a finding about the docs quality, not a skill failure.

SKILL.md:850:2. **Phase B — Requirement derivation.** Read CONTRACTS.md and documentation. Group related contracts, enrich with user intent, write formal requirements.
